Representatives of the Ministry of Panchayati Raj 
 
1. Smt. L.M.Vas   - Secretary  
2. Shri Hrusikesh Panda  - Additional Secretary  
3. Shri G. C.Saini   - Joint Director 
 
2. At the outset, Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the members to the sitting of the 
Committee convened to hear the views/suggestions of the representatives of the 
Ministry of Agriculture on the various provisions of the ' The National Food Security Bill, 
2011'. Thereafter, the Secretary and other officials of the Ministry of Agriculture and the 
Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution (Department of Food and 
Public Distribution) were invited to the sitting of the Committee.  After welcoming them, 
the Hon‘ble Chairman apprised them of the provisions of Direction 55(1) of the 
Directions by the Speaker. The Committee, then, heard the views/suggestions of the 
representatives of the Ministry of Agriculture on the various provision s of 'The National 
Food Security Bill, 2011'.  
 
3. The Secretary, Ministry of Agriculture then presented the views//suggestions of 
the Ministry on the various provisions of the Bill. Some of the important points that 
emerged during the deliberations of the Committee are given as under:- 
 
(i) Need to address the issue of tackling the problem of food and 
nutritional security for the entire citizenry.  
 
(ii) Need to assess the requirements of demand for various food crops 
in order to increase production and productivity accordingly. 
  
(iii) Need to persuade farmers to cultivate multiple crops even in non -
traditional areas as done, for instance, in the states of Chhattisgarh, 
Madhya Pradesh in order to increase production and procurement. 
(iv) The expert group constituted by the Pl anning Commission has 
estimated the demand of cereals at the end of 12th Plan to the tune 
of 235 MT and estimated supply in the range of 240 to 250 MT. So 
country is in a position to meet the required demand if estimates 
are pegged at 235 MT.  
  

 
 
 
(v) As per est imation of Department of Food and Public Distribution, 
the requirement of foodgrains after the passage of the Bill would be 
around 70 million tonnes or so and as per current estimate, there 
would be shortage of about 14 -15 million tonnes of foodgrains 
procurement.  
 
(vi) Due to PDS, everybody consumes wheat and rice despite the fact 
that coarse cereals have good nutritional value and sustainability 
owing to less water requirement for irrigation. 
 
(vii) Need to think of ways to have sustainable production and 
incentivize production of pulses and oil seeds. 
 
(viii) There is a need to have procurement in each State. 
 
(ix) Need to diversify and increase the production of rice in other areas 
of the country to bring to the level of Punjab and Haryana.  
 
(x) There is an extreme poverty and s mall land holdings in Eastern 
India. Therefore, there is a need to centralize all the interventions in 
Eastern India in order to diversify the agriculture.  
 
(xi) Need to establish equilibrium over two opposing practices likely to 
be observed after implementing  the Bill as well as increasing 
procurement so as to control prices of the commodities. 
 
(xii) Need for water -harvesting and water conservation especially in 
villages to avoid wastage of water by encouraging sprinklers and 
drip farming and creating ponds, lakes etc. 
 
(xiii) Need to focus on changing cropping pattern, diversified production, 
integrated agriculture in which animal husbandry have important 
contribution. 
 
(xiv) Need to encourage and develop seeds which are flood and drought 
resistant as done by International Rice Research Institute. 
 
(xv) There is a strong need to prevent diversion of agricultural land for 
non-agricultural purpose as well as giving rest for 3 to  4 seasons 
before next sowing to prevent decline in factor of productivity.  
 
4. The representatives of the Ministry responded to the queries raised by the 
Chairman and the members on the aforesaid issues. 
 
5. The Hon‘ble Chairman then thanked the Secretary and other representatives of 
the Ministry of Agriculture for their free and frank discussion.  
  
(The Witnesses then withdrew) 

 
 
 
 
[The representatives of the Ministry of Panchayati Raj were then called in] 
 
6. After welcoming them, the Hon‘ble Chairman apprised them of the provisions of 
Direction 55(1) of the Directions by the Speaker. 
 
7. Thereafter, the Secret ary, Ministry of Panchayati Raj explained the 
views/suggestions of the Ministry on the various provisions of 'The National Food 
Security Bill, 2011'. He also explained the various concerns and issues raised by 
Hon‘ble Chairman and supplemented by the Membe rs of the Committee.  Some of the 
important points that emerged during the deliberations of the Committee are given as 
under:- 
(i) There is a strong need to define Gram Sabha under Clause 2 for the 
purpose of Local Self Government where panchayats are func tioning, in 
the Special PESA area where the Scheduled Tribes exist and in the North-
East where there are no panchayats. 
 
(ii) Need for arrangements through the local authorities for persons who are 
living in starvation. Therefore, involvement of Gram Sabha  for 
identification of the priority households. 
 
(iii) The power for the inclusion or exclusion under the list should vest with the 
Gram Sabha. However, final acceptance of the list can always vest in the 
State Government. 
 
(iv) Licensing function should r emain with the Local Self -Government bodies 
like the Panchayat or the municipalities or the elected village councils. 
 
(v) An elected woman member of the district panchayat and in urban districts 
where the panchayats do not exist, elected women member of t he 
municipality be designated as the ex -officio Additional District Grievance 
Redressal Officer.  
 
8. The representatives of the Ministry responded to the queries raised by the 
Chairman and the members on the aforesaid issues. 
 
 
9. The Hon‘ble Chairman the n thanked the Secretary and other representatives of 
the Ministry of Panchayati Raj for their free and frank discussion.  
 
  The Committee then adjourned. 
  A verbatim record of the proceedings has been kept.

children's access to food.

2. At the outset, the Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the Members to the sitting of the 
Committee which was convened to hear the views/suggestions of the representatives of 
the Ministries of (i) Social Justice & Empowerment (D/o Social Justice & Empowerment 
and D/o Disability Affairs) , (ii)  Railways, and (iii) Tribal Affairs  on the various issues 
relating to  ‗The National Food Security Bill, 2011‘, which i s under examination by the 
Committee. 
 
[The witnesses from Ministry of Social Justice & Empowerment were called in] 
 
3. Thereafter, Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the representatives of  Ministry of 
Social Justice & Empowerment to the sitting of the Committee and r equested them to 
be free and frank while giving their views/suggestions on the various 
Chapter/Provisions/Clauses of the aforesaid Bill, which after its enactment is going to 
address the food problems of underprivileged/deprived lot of the nation. They wer e also 
requested to keep the discussions confidential until the Report of the Committee is 
submitted to the Parliament.  
  

 
 
 
4. The representatives of the Ministry briefed the Committee about their target 
groups, who are a deprived lot, namely, Scheduled Cas tes, the Other Backward 
Classes, Senior Citizens, People with Disabilities and Victims of Drug Abuse and 
Alcoholism and requested the Committee to consider the inclusion of BPL members 
amongst their target groups in the category of Priority Households.  Ap art from this, the 
following are some of the important points that emerged during the deliberations of the 
Committee: 
 
i. that there is need to make a provision that every girl child belonging to 
BPL should get one free meal per day irrespective of the fact w hether she 
goes to school or not, because in our society there is a sense of 
discrimination exercised by the parents between a 'girl child' and a 'boy 
child' and unless the 'girl child' gets the food and able to grow up properly, 
she cannot become a proper mother; and 
 
ii. that there is need to make provision to provide free food/ration 
commodities to about 2.2 crore physically and mentally disabled children 
and adults in the BPL families as they have not been given food owing to 
discrimination by the abled in the families, and very often treated as 
unwanted burden  and thus remained ignored and neglected.  
 
5. Chairman and Members of the Committee raised queries during the deliberation 
which were replied to by the representatives of the Ministry.  Chairman then thanked the 
witnesses for sharing their valuable views with the Committee. 
 
[The witnesses then withdrew.] 
[The representatives of the Ministry of Railways (Railway Board) were then called in] 
 
6. In the absence of Chairman, Railway Board without prior in timation/taking prior 
permission for his leave of absence from the Hon'ble Chairman of the Committee, the 
Committee decided not to interact with the representatives of the Railway Board and 
desired to convey to him that the Committee took strong objection to it and that he 
should appear before the Committee when the Committee desire. 
 
[The witnesses then withdrew.] 
[Tea Break] 
[After Tea Break, the representatives of Ministry of Tribal Affairs were called in] 
 
7. Hon‘ble Chairman of the Committee welcomed t he representatives of Ministry of 
Tribal Affairs.  He also mentioned about Direction 55(1) of the Direction by the Speaker 
to keep the discussions confidential till the Report of the Committee is presented to the 
Parliament. 
 
8. The representatives of the  Ministry of Tribal Affairs briefly stated that food 
security is a vital concern for tribals in the Scheduled Areas.  Food Security in 
Scheduled Areas is especially fragile because of primitive agricultural practices, low 
production, difficult access, poor infrastructure and logical services, weak governance, 

 
 
 
endemic poverty and lack of opportunities for livelihood maintenance.  Therefore, both 
availability of food grains and affordability are inter -meshed problems and food security 
is not merely a question of subsidizing the prices for the poor sections of the people in 
these areas.  Thus, there is a compelling reason for recognizing these special 
characteristics and making special provisions for Tribals in Scheduled Areas as well as 
other special pockets having concentration of tribal population for special dispensations/ 
inclusion in the special category.  Apart from this, some of the important points that 
emerged out of deliberations of the Committee are as under:-   
(i) that there is need for providing foodgr ains and millets and ragi, etc. 
for food security and nutritional security of Tribals; 
 
(ii) that Direct Cash Transfer will not be adequate and appropriate 
arrangement for Tribals; 
 
(iii) that as a matter of fact, the very criteria of determining the BPL 
itself is no t realistic and seldom reflects the ground reality, 
particularly, with respect to tribals and forest dwellers, hence, BPL 
cannot be used as the sole yardstick for inclusion or exclusion of 
Tribals for providing BPL benefits to them.  Otherwise, as per 
norms of the socio -economic caste census, tribal families having 
large chunks of barren/unproductive lands would continue to 
remain excluded from the list of BPL Category.  Hence, the need 
for creating a really meaningful criteria for Scheduled Areas and 
Tribal families; and 
 
(iv) that the entitlements of Tribals should not be lapsed because they 
are unable to take these in one or two months, hence, need to 
make enabling provision in the Bill to carry forward the entitlements 
of Tribals to be availed at a later point of time.  
   
9. The queries raised by the Hon'ble Chairman and Members during the course of 
deliberations were replied to by the representatives of the Ministry. Hon‘ble Chairman 
then thanked the witnesses for sharing their views with the Committee.  
[The witnesses then withdrew.] 
 A verbatim record of the proceedings has been kept. 
 
The Committee then adjourned to meet after Lunch.

Ministry of Finance 
(i) (Department of Expenditure) (1500 hrs. to 1635 hrs.) 
1. Shri R.S.Gujral - FS & Secretary(E) 
2. Ms.Anjuly Chib Duggal - Additional Secretary(E) 
3. Dr.Saurabh Garg - Joint Secretary (PF.II) 
 
 (ii) (Department of Economic Affairs) 
 
1. Shri Arvind Mayaram - Secretary(Economic Affairs)

3. Shri M. C. Singhi - Sr. Economic Adviser 
 
[The Committee re-assembled after Lunch] 
[The witnesses from Ministry of Finance (Department of Expenditure 
and Department of Economic Affairs) were called in] 
  
2. The Hon‘ble Chairman of the Committee welcomed the representatives of the 
Ministry of Finance (D/o Expenditure and D/o Economic Affairs) and requested them to 
be free and frank while presenting their views before the Committee and also maintain 
the confidentiality of the discussions made during the sitting of the Committee till the 
Report of the Committee on ‗The National Food Security Bill, 2011‘ is presented to the 
Parliament as required under Direction 55(1) of the 'The Directions by the Speaker, Lok 
Sabha'. 
 
3. Thereafter, the rep resentatives of  both the Departments of the Ministry of 
Finance briefed the Committee about their views/suggestions on the various aspects of 
‗The National Food Security Bill, 2011‘.  Hon'ble Chairman and Members of the 
Committee also raised queries which were replied to by representatives of both the 
Departments. Some of the important issues/points that emerged out of the interactive 
session are as under: 
 
(i) that there are predominantly three subsidies, namely, petroleum subsidy, 
food subsidy and the fertilizer subsidy.  Government would be conscious 
to ensure that it is within the manageable fiscal limits of the Government.  
The manageable fiscal limit which is bearable by the economy and 
budgetary process has been assessed to two percent of GDP; 
 
(ii) Large sect ions of people/voters are not happy with the definition and 
inclusion criteria of BPL category and aggrieved people are demanding 
flexible norms/reformative changes in the criteria for BPL list;  
 
(iii)  Fair and just inclusion of deserving persons and exclusion  of 
undeserving persons from the BPL list is essentially required;  
 
(iv) Reforms in TPDS is a must and needs to be done emphatically on 
priority; 
 

 
 
 
(v) End to end computerization of TPDS to avoid misuse of TPDS 
commodities needed to be done; 
 
(vi) Need to cover poor peo ple for providing legal right to food as well as 
foodgrains, etc. in the beginning and gradually after establishing success 
of first phase of implementation of the provisions of the proposed law, 
others need to be covered depending upon production and proc urement 
of foodgrains, etc; 
 
(vii) that there should not be many criteria, rather only two sustainable 
criteria, one which is absolutely mandatory and essential for inclusion in 
the BPL category and the other to the extent to have flexibility based 
upon what the  Government can cover for providing food and nutritional 
security;  
 
(viii) that the Bill has the enabling provisions for direct cash subsidy transfer 
to the beneficiaries of BPL in the long run but not immediately and that is 
appropriate as per the representatives of Ministry of Finance; and  
 
(ix) The benefits of cash subsidy transfer is that it gives an additional option 
to the individual that one need not take foodgrains only from PDS and 
one can go even in private shops to get it.  Moreover, cost of 
transportation, storage, leakages and spoilages are very high in the 
system of PDS and balance of convenience and economy is to be seen. 
 
4. The queries raised by the Hon'ble Chairman and Members during the 
deliberations were replied to by the representatives of the two  Departments. Hon‘ble 
Chairman then thanked the representatives for sharing their views before the 
Committee.  
[The witnesses then withdrew.] 
 
[The Committee then adjourned.] 
 
A verbatim record of the proceedings has been kept.

SECRETARIAT 
 
     1. Shri P. K. Misra  - Joint Secretary  
2. Smt. Veena Sharma  - Director 
3. Shri Khakhai Zou  - Under Secretary 
 
 

 
 
 
 All India Democratic Women's Association   
 
1. Ms. Sudha Sundararaman - General Secretary  
2. Ms. Jagmati Sangwan - Member 
3. Ms. Smita Gupta - Member 
 
 
Ministry of  Health and Family Welfare  
 
1. Shri P.K. Pradhan - Secretary (H&FW) 
2. Ms. Anudradha Gupta - Addl. Secretary &M.D, NRHM 
3. Shri Rakesh Kumar - Joint Secretary 
4. Dr. Ajay Khera - Deputy Commissioner 
5. Dr. B.K. Tiwari  - Adviser 
 
2. At the outset, the Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the Members to the sitting of the 
Committee which was convened to hear the views/suggestions of the representatives of 
(i) All India Democratic Women's Association and  (ii) Ministry of Health and Family 
Welfare on the various provisions of ‗The National Food Security  Bill, 2011‘. 
[The witnesses from All India Democratic Women's Association were called in] 
 
3. Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the representatives of All India Democratic 
Women's Association to the sitting and mentioned, inter -alia, that the discussions held 
shall be kept confidential as required under the provisions of Direction 55(1) of the 
Directions by the Speaker and requested t hem to share their views/suggestions on any 
chapter, clause or provision of the National Food Security Bill, 2011 which they feel 
should be considered by the Committee in their report.  
 
4. Thereafter, the representatives of All India Democratic Women's Association 
tendered their views/suggestions on the Bill. Some of the important points that emerged 
during the deliberations of the Committee are as under:- 
 
(i) The right to food must be a universal right. 
 
(ii) The approach to tackle hunger and malnutrition, in the context of rising 
food prices, is to universalize and strengthen the Public Distribution 
System by making adequate food available at affordable prices. 
 
(iii) The performanc e evaluation of the earlier targeted PDS, conducted by 
Planning Commission, showed that 43 percent of the intended 
beneficiaries under BPL were excluded from its benefits due to wrong 
identification procedures which was cumbersome and bureaucratic. 
 
(iv) In States like Tamil Nadu, Andhra Pradesh, Kerala, Chhatisgarh that have 
near universal or a larger coverage than the TPDS, the leakages have 
been actually comparatively lower than other States.  Therefore, a strong 

 
 
 
universal PDS is the best method to ensure foo d security and also deal 
with the problems of leakages and exclusion.  
 
(v) The Bill does not simply replace the earlier APL and BPL categories into 
priority and general households.  It adds further categories which leads to 
endless identification errors. 
 
(vi)  Where there is a problem in implementing universalisation of PDS, the 
income tax payees can be excluded.  
 
(vii) While dealing with expansion of entitlements there is a need to look at 
whether the Bill will provide the basic food security that is necessary for 
the families in our country.  
 
(viii) A minimum monthly entitlement of 7 kg. or 35 kg per person whichever is 
more, including coarse grain and one kg. pulses, one kg. edible oil and 
half kg. sugar per person in all households under the proposed National 
Food Security Act would be a very essential way to provide Food Security.  
 
(ix) Need to improve storage capacity to prevent damage and rottening of 
foodgrains. 
 
(x) Failure of earlier schemes like Ladli etc. while taking into consideration the 
number of Banks, infrastructure and manpower involved in the country, 
the workability of cash transfer in the Bill is little doubtful.  Hence, this 
provision should be removed from the Bill.   
 
(xi) Need to decentralize and maximize procurement. 
(xii) While taking into consideration the production level, MSP should be 
declared in advance.  
 
(xiii) Need to have awareness campaign making poor and illiterate section 
aware of the rules regarding food security so as to prevent them from 
being duped by intermediators. 
  
[The witnesses then withdrew.] 
[The representatives of the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare were then called in] 
  
5. Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the representatives of the Ministry of Health and 
Family Welfare and requested them to brief the Committee about the views/suggestions 
of the Ministr y on the Bill.  He also mentioned that under the provisions of Direction 
55(1) of the Directions by the Speaker, the discussions held shall be kept confidential till 
the Report of the Committee is presented to the Parliament.   
 
6. Thereafter, the Secretar y tendered the views of the Ministry on the various 
provisions of the Bill before the Committee.  The following points emerged during the 
deliberations:- 

 
 
 
 
(i) Ensuring the Food Security will definitely help in improving the health 
indicators. 
 
(ii) Maternity benefit of Rs. 1000 per month for a period of six months is 
equally relevant both during pregnancy and also for the next six months.  
The Bill should also provide for flexibility to the Government to revise the 
said amount as and when it is felt necessary. 
 
(iii) Need to re -consider the proviso of Clause 4 of the Bill, which exclude all 
pregnant women and lactating mothers in regular employment with 
Central Government, State Government or Public Sector undertaking from 
availing benefits under the Bill. 
 
(iv) Taking the financial implications into consideration rather than giving 
amount of Rs. 1000 for sixth month, Rs. 500 can be given for a year. 
 
(v) Janani Sishu Suraksha Scheme have been started in which any pregnant 
mother and the sick neonates are ent itled for free service like free drugs, 
free diagnosis, free diet and also free transportation from home to 
institution and back.  Grievances redressal mechanism is also being 
established. 
 
(vi) The rate at which foodgrains will be provided to priority hou seholds may 
be revised from time to time. 
 
(vii) Need to upgrade district hospitals to Multi -specialty Care Centres, while 
also providing for physical infrastructure, human resources and specialists 
in district hospitals.  
 
(viii) Need to have proper check s and control and transparency in terms of 
expenditure involved in various health treatments. 
 
 (ix) Clinical Establishment Bill which has been brought by the Ministry and is 
implemented in some States.  The Bill provides that all the clinics should 
be registered.  
 
7. The queries raised by the Chairman and Members of the Committee during the 
course of the deliberation were responded to by the representatives of the Ministry of 
Health and Family Welfare. The Hon‘ble Chairman then thanked the witnesses for 
appearing before the Committee and sharing their views in a free and frank manner. 
  
[The witnesses then withdrew.] 
 A verbatim record of the proceedings has been kept. 
 
The Committee then adjourned to meet after lunch break.

MINUTES OF THE SITTING OF THE STANDING COMMITTEE ON FOOD,

     1. Shri P. K. Misra - Joint Secretary

(i) Unique Identification Authority of India  
1.  Shri Ram Sewak Sharma - Director General & Mission Director 
2. Shri Kumar Alok - Deputy Director General 
3. Shri M.S. Seetharaman - Deputy Director General 
4. Shri R.K. Gautam  - Assistant Director General 
 
(ii) National Infomatics Centre  
1. Dr. B.K. Gairole  - Director General 
2. Dr. Y.K.Sharma - Deputy Director General 
3. Dr. (Mrs.) Ranjna Nagpal - Sr. Tech Director 
 
[The witnesses from Unique Identification Authority of India and  National 
Informatics Centre were called in] 
 
2. Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the representatives of the Unique Identification 
Authority of India and National Informatics Centre  and requested them to be free and 
frank while presenting their views before the Committee and also maintain the 
confidentiality of the discussions held during the sitting of the Committee till the Report 
of the Committee on the National Food Security Bill, 2011 is presented to the 
Parliament as required under the provisions of Direction 55(1) of th e Directions by the 
Speaker Lok Sabha. 
3. Thereafter, the representatives of Unique Identification Authority of India and  
National Informatics Centre tendered  their views/suggestions on the various aspects of 
'The National Food Security Bill, 2011'. Some o f the important issues/points that 
emerged during the deliberations of the Committee are as under:- 
 
(a) There are few stages at which Unique Identification Authority of India 
(UIDAI)/NIC can help the Government in proper implementation of this bill viz. 
 
(i) at t he point of identification of beneficiary by ensuring Unique 
identification given to every individual. 
(ii) at the time of delivery of foodgrains and other related items to the 
beneficiaries by providing method of authenticating the identity online.  
(iii) Portability of entitlements: since ADHAAR is a portable identification 
infrastructure which can be verified online from any place.  
 
(b) NIC has been supporting the Department of Food and Public Distribution at 
the Central/State level for the last 7 -8 years and helped i n producing 
technology at different stage of the entire distribution chain. 
 
(c) Need for digitization of Ration Card through integration of ADHAAR into the 
ration card system. 
 

 
 
 
(d) Need to track actual transaction of giving ration to the beneficiary.  This 
involves supply-chain management and track transaction at the shop as well 
as  transaction done by the shop and its delivery to actual beneficiary.   
 
(e) For proper networking there is a need for better institutional structure to 
support strengthening of ICT component.  
 
(f) A task force constituted by the Ministry of Finance under the Chairman of 
UIDAI, Shri Nandan Nilekani with representatives from all the 
stakeholders/Ministries to suggest technology architecture for managing the 
entire PDS System had submitted its R eport in October, 2011 recommending 
inter-alia for having a Public Distribution System network to be managed by 
Centre with the co -operation of State Governments which are at different 
levels of IT introduction into the PDS.   
 
(g) Adhaar Project ensures inclu sion and identity to those who do not have one 
by carrying out a special camp for certain disadvantage groups in the society 
viz. pavement dwellers or who are in the orphanages, etc. 
 
(h) Adhaar does not give card like smart card but seeks to provide identity which 
is verifiable online. 
 
4. The queries raised by the Hon'ble Chairman and Members during the 
deliberations were replied to by the representatives of Unique Identification Authority of 
India and  National Informatics Centre . Hon‘ble Chairman then thanke d the 
representatives for sharing their views before the Committee.  
 
[The witnesses then withdrew.] 
 
[The Committee then adjourned.] 
 
A verbatim record of the proceedings has been kept.

III. Representatives of Right To Food Campaign -Steering Committee:

2. At the outset, the Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the Members of the Committee to 
the sitting convened to hear the views and suggestions of the representatives of Right 
to Food Campaign on the various provisions of ‗The National Food Security Bill, 2011‘ 
and to consider and adopt the draft Report of the Committee (2012 -13) on the action 
taken by the Government on the observations/recommendations contained in the 
Eighteenth Report of the Committee (2011 -12) on DFG (2012 -13) pertaining to the 
Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution (Department of Consumer 
Affairs) 
  [The witnesses were then called in] 
 
3. The Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the representatives of Right to Food Campaign 
to the sitting and mentioned, inter -alia, that the discussions held shall be kept 
confidential as required under the provisions of Direction 55(1 ) of the Directions by the 
Speaker and requested t he representatives of the Right to Food Campaign  to share 
their views/suggestions on any chapter, clause or provision of the National Food 
Security Bill, 2011 which they felt should be considered by the Committee in their report. 
Thereafter the representatives briefed the Committee about the views/suggestions of 
the organization on the Bill. The following are some of the important points that 
emerged during the deliberations of the Committee:- 
 
(i) Need to reduc e the delegated legislation as regards the Bill to ensure 
proper implementation; 
(ii) Need to widen the scope of the Bill and universalize the entitlement under 
the Bill eliminating the three categories of excluded, general and priority; 
(iii) Need to delink the Mini mum Support Price and Public Distribution Price of 
entitlements to general category households; 
(iv) Need to include other commodities such as oil, pulses, sugar etc. to the 
goods to be provided ;  
(v) Need to provide 50% of the monthly minimum wages to the pregnan t 
ladies to reduce the pressure of work and to provide social security ; and 
(vi) Non suitability of cash transfer in lieu of foodgrains; etc. 
  

 
 
 
4. The queries raised by the Chairman and Members of the Committee during the 
course of the deliberation were responded to by the representatives of the Right to Food 
Campaign. The Hon‘ble Chairman then thanked the witnesses for appearing before the 
Committee and sharing their views with them.  
 
[The witnesses then withdrew.] 
 
5. The Hon‘ble Chairman then highlighted th e important recommendations 
contained in the draft Report of the Committee (2012 -13) on the action taken by the 
Government on the observations/recommendations contained in the Eighteenth Report 
of the Committee (2011 -12) on DFG (2012 -13) pertaining to the Ministry of Consumer 
Affairs, Food and Public Distribution (Department of Consumer Affairs). The Committee 
then took up for consideration the above stated draft Report. After due deliberation, the 
Committee unanimously adopted the draft Report without any 
amendments/modifications.  
6.  The Committee then authorized the Chairman to finalize the aforesaid Report 
and present the same to the Parliament. 
 
 A verbatim record of the proceedings has been kept. 
 
The Committee then adjourned.

Ministry of Women and Child Development  
1. Shri Prem Narain   - Secretary  
2. Dr. Shreeranjan  - Joint Secretary

Ministry Of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution 
(Department of Food and Public Distribution) 
 
1. Shri Sudhir Kumar - Secretary

2. At the outset, Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the Members to the sitting of the 
Committee which was convened to hear the views/suggestions of the representatives of 
the Ministry of Women and Child Development)  on ‗The National Food Security Bill, 
2011‘, in the presence of the officials of the Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and 
Public Distribution (Department of Food and Public Distribution. 
 
[The witnesses from Ministry of Women and Child Development and 
representatives of the Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution 
(Department of Food and Public Distribution) were called in] 
 
3. Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the representatives of  Ministry of Women and 
Child Devel opment and Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution 
(Department of Food and Public Distribution)  to the sitting of the Committee and 
requested them to be free and frank while expressing their views/suggestions on the 
various Chapter/Provi sions/Clauses of the aforesaid Bill, that is going to address the 
food problems of underprivileged/deprived lot of the nation after its enactment. They 
were also requested to keep the discussions confidential as required under the 
provisions of Direction 5 5(1) of the Directions by the Speaker, until the Report of the 
Committee is submitted to the Parliament.  
  
4. Thereafter, the representatives of the Ministry of Women and Child Development 
briefed the Committee about their views/suggestions on the various  provision of the 
National Food Security Bill, 2011.  Some of the important points that emerged during 
the deliberations of the Committee are: 
 
(i) Restructured and strengthened ICDS will take some time to adhere to the 
entitlements under the provisions of  the Bill, for being actually 
implemented on the ground; 
 

 
 
 
(ii) Need to prevent slippage of supply of supplementary food given and to 
develop a full -proof system to ensure that each and every  child that is 
coming under ICDS centre gets the entitlement as provided in the Act; 
 
(iii) Need to monitor the functioning of anganwadi centres so that no such 
centres remain without anganwadi workers; 
 
(iv) Problems envisaged in the implementation of the Bill which are of 
administrative and managerial nature can be surmounted; 
 
(v) Apart from ICDS, the Department is running SABLA which is a nutrition 
based Scheme for increasing nutritional status of adolescent girls as a 
part of Rajiv Gandhi Empowerment Scheme for Adolescent Girls- between 
11 and 18 years which provide s take home ration and is operative in 205 
districts; and 
 
(vi) The provision under Clause 4(b) regarding maternity benefits of Rs. 1000 
per month for a period of six months is based on another pilot scheme 
known as Indira Gandhi Matritva Sahyog Yogana (IG MSY).  Therefore, 
this provision should not be included in the act.  This should be left to the 
Ministries of the Central Government. 
 
5. Queries raised by the Hon'ble Chairman and Members of the Committee during 
the deliberation were replied to by the rep resentatives of the Ministry.  Chairman then 
thanked the witnesses for sharing their valuable views with the Committee. 
 
[The witnesses then withdrew.] 
 A verbatim record of the proceedings has been kept. 
The Committee then adjourned.

1. Shri Sudhir Kumar  - Secretary

representatives to share their views/suggestions on any chapter, clause or provisions of 
the National Food Security Bill, 2011 which they felt should be considered by the 
Committee in their report. Thereafter the r epresentatives of the Ministry of Railways 
briefed the Committee about the views/suggestions of the Ministry on the Bill. The 
following are some of the important points that emerged during the deliberations of the 
Committee:- 
 
(i) Need to have better coordinat ion and cooperation in the operations of the 
Department of Food and Public Distribution, FCI and the Ministry of 
Railways; 
 
(ii) Need for making special arrangements to facilitate the additional 
requirements arising due to the implementation of the Bill; and 
 
(iii) Need to ensure cleanliness and hygiene of the wagons which carry food 
grains and fix its responsibility on the rightful authorities;etc.

course of the deliberation were responded  to by the representatives of the Ministry of

mentioned that under the provisions of Direction 55(1) of the Directions by the Speaker, 
the discussions held shall be confidential before the Committee present  their report to 
the Parliament. 
 
6. Thereafter, Smt. Brinda Karat, Ex. MP, (RS) tendered her views/suggestions on 
the various provisions of the Bill. Some of the important points that emerged during the 
deliberations of the Committee are as follows:- 
(i) Need to have a single exclusion criteria and entitle all others to the 
benefits under the Bill; 
 
(ii) Need to include pulses and edible oil in the list of goods to be provided; 
(iii) Need to fix financial responsibility for implementation of the Bill on the 
Central Gover nment and if any scheme is to be implemented on cost 
sharing basis it should only be done in consultation with the State 
Governments; 
 
(iv) Deletion of Clauses related to cash transfers, food coupons and clause 52 
which allows suspension of the food security due to force majeure; and 

 
 
 
(v) Need to provide hot cooked meals instead of ready to eat meals to 
children, pregnant and lactating women, destitute and people in starvation; 
etc. 
 
7. Smt. Brinda Karat, Ex. MP (RS) responded to the queries raised by the Chairman 
and the members on the aforesaid issues. 
 
8. The Hon‘ble Chairman then thanked her for appearing before the Committee and 
sharing her views with them in a free and frank manner.  
[The witness then withdrew.]

 2. At the outset, the Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the Members of the Committee to 
the sitting convened to hear the views and suggestion Prof. M. S. Swaminathan,  M.P., 
Rajya Sabha and Chairman, M. S. Swaminathan Research Foundation  on the various 
provisions of ‗The National Food Security Bill, 2011‘ and to consider and adopt the draft 
Report of the Committee (2012 -13) on the action taken by the Government on the 
observations/recommendations contained in the Seventeenth Report of the Committee 
(2011-12) on DFG (2012 -13) pertaining to the Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and 
Public Distribution (Department of Food and Public Distribution).  
[Prof. M. S. Swaminathan, M.P., Rajya Sabha was then called in] 
3. The Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed Prof. M. S. Swaminathan, M.P., Rajya Sabha 
and Chairman, M. S. Swaminathan Research Foundation  to the sitting and mentioned, 
inter-alia, that the discussions held shall be kept confidential as required under the 
provisions of Direction 55(1) of the Directions by the Speaker and requested him  to 
share his views/suggestions on any chapter, clause or provision of the National Food 
Security Bill, 2011 which he felt should be considered by the Committee in their report. 
Thereafter Prof. M. S. Swaminathan, M.P., Rajya Sabha briefed the Committee about 
his views/suggestions on the Bill. The following are some of the important points that 
emerged during the deliberations of the Committee:- 
(i) Need to address the problem of malnutrition in the country by taking 
concrete steps; 
 
(ii) Importance of availability of food  in the Market, access to food, and 
absorption of food in the body; 
 
(iii) Need to protect entitlement of beneficiaries under Antyodaya Anna 
Yojana; 
 
(iv) Need to adopt variable exclusion formula for deciding coverage in different 
States and Union Territories ;  
 
(v) Need to include one male and one female farmers in the National and 
State Food Commissions to get first hand information directly from the 
producers and largest consumers;  
 
(vi) Need to procure and distribute bajra, ragi, minor millets like madhuva and 
makki unde r the PDS to combat the predicted downfall in production of 
Wheat; and 
 
(vii) Need to recommend and monitor effective implementation of National 
Policy for Farmers, Rajiv Gandhi Drinking Water Mission, Mahatma 
Gandhi Total Sanitation Programme, The Rural and Urb an Health 
Missions, etc. to ensure actual food security; etc.  
  

 
 
 
4. The queries raised by the Chairman and Members of the Committee during the 
course of the deliberation were responded to by Prof. M. S. Swaminathan, M.P., Rajya 
Sabha. The Hon‘ble Chairman then thanked him for appearing before the Committee 
and sharing his views with them.

Representatives of the Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public 
Distribution (Department of Food and Public Distribution) 
 
1. Shri Sudhir Kumar - Secretary

3. Shri Deepak Kumar - Joint Secretary

5. Shri Nilambuj Sharan - Director 
 
Food Corporation of India (FCI) 
 
1. Dr. Amar Singh - Chairman and Managing Director 
2. Shri Dinesh Kumar - Executive Director 
 
Central Warehousing Corporation (CWC) 
 
1. Shri B. B. Pattanaik - Managing Director 
 
  
2. At the outset, the Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the Members to the sitting of the 
Committee.  The Committee then invited the representatives of the Department of Food 
and Public Distribution to the sitting of the Committee to take oral evidence on 'The 
National Food Security Bill, 2011' and apprised them of the provisions of Direction 55(1) 
of the Directions by the Speaker.   
 
3. Thereafter the Secretary, Department of Food and Public Distribution explained 
the various issues related to the provisions of t he Bill and also addressed the concerns 
raised by the Hon'ble Chairman in his opening remarks.  The following are some of the 
important  points that emerged during the sitting of the Committee: 
(i) As the progress of survey conducted by SECC regarding selectio n of 
beneficiaries is not as per expectation, the only alternative available is to 
leave the selection criteria with the States/UTs; 
(ii) Too many categorization has created problems.  Therefore, need to have 
only one universal category;  
(iii) Need to have some guidelines for making exclusion criteria; 
(iv) Taking into consideration increasing population every year, provision of 
revising list of beneficiaries every year is not possible.  Therefore, need to 
extend the period of revision; 
(v) Entitlement of 5 kg. per beneficiar y is manageable for providing food on 
sustainable basis; 
(vi) Need to ensure and monitor take home food facility provided in 
Anganwadis for small children; 

 
 
 
(vii) Need to clearly define that children in a family are also taken into account 
while preparing list of beneficiaries; 
(viii) Direct Cash Transfer at the initial stage of the scheme should be taken as 
a pilot project; and 
(ix) Without providing for nutrition security there is no relevance of food 
security in the country.    
4. Hon'ble Chairman thanked the Secretary and othe r representatives for their free 
and frank discussion.

2.  At the outset, the Hon‘ble Chairman welcomed the Members of the Committee 
to the sitting conv ened to consider and adopt the draft Report of the Committee    
(2012-13) on the National Food Security Bill, 2011 pertaining to the Ministry of 
Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution (Department of Food and Public 
Distribution).  

 
 
 
3. In his opening  remarks, Hon‘ble Chairman highlighted the important 
recommendations contained in the draft Report and requested the Members to suggest 
amendment/modification, if any, in the recommendations. Thereafter, after due 
deliberation, the committee adopted the Dr aft Report with slight 
amendments/modifications. However, one of the Members of the Committee viz.        
Dr. T. N. Seema, did not agree to one or two recommendations and stated that she may 
give a note of dissent subsequently.

and present the same to the Hon‘ble Speaker, Lok Sabha. 
 
 A verbatim record of the proceedings has been kept. 
 
The Committee then adjourned.

Appendix XXIV 
NOTE OF DISSENT 
DR. T.N. SEEMA, M.P. (RS) 
 I wish to record my disagreement and dissent on some recommendations of the 
Committee as well as on some recommendations not made by the Committee which I 
feel should have been made.  I have expressed these views in the course of the 
discussions in the Committee also.  However the final report in my opinion is not 
satisfactory on these issues fundamental to the question of food security, thus this note 
of dissent. 
1. The entitlements in the Bill should be universal in nature with no caps artificially  
decided.  However the Committee has recommended only a uniform entitlement not a 
universal one.  Thus the numbers of those to be covered by even a uniform entitlement 
will be subject to the arbitrary caps put by the Planning Commission of 75 per cent 
(rural) and 50 per cent (urban).  In fact the Committee specifically recommends these 
caps in para 2.5.  This undermines food security in a fundamental way.  The 
recommendation of the Committee is only that the State Governments if they want can 
increase the c overage at their own cost.  This is both meaningless and unfair.  At 
present the Bill specifically states that all foodgrains provided by the centre to the States 
have to be distributed as mandated. This means that the Sates which are using the 
foodgrains at present to ensure near universal coverage will no longer be able to do so.  
They will mean the elimination of the effective food programmes being run by several 
State Governments.  The Committee has failed to take these factors into account. 
2. The unif orm entitlement recommended by the Committee at 5 kg per head is 
quite unacceptable as it would reduce even the present entitlement for BPL and AAY 
families of 35 kgs effectively by 10 kgs for a family of five.  This cut in entitlement will be 
creating food insecurity for 6.52 crore families (presently identified as BPL/Antodaya).  
This will only help the Government to contain its subsidy but not provide food security 
which is the aim of the Bill.  It would have been better to have kept the entitlement to 7  
kgs per individual for all with a minimum of 35 kgs ensured by law.  Larger families 
would in any case be covered through the individual entitlement. 
3. The Committee has correctly recommended a uniform price for all.  However it 
has adopted the prices of  three rupees, two rupees and one rupee, for one kg of rice, 
wheat and millets respectively.  While this is beneficial for those who would have been 
categorized as ―general sections‖ in the Government Bill, it is not doing justice to the 
Antodaya sections who are getting their allotments at two rupees per kg. which has also 
been adopted as the price norm  for non -Antodaya sections in many States.  Thus the 
committee‘s recommendation should have been taken into account the present situation 
in the States and suggested a uniform price of 35 kgs(minimum) at two rupee a kilo. 
4. The Bill has been rightly criticized by many of the representations being highly 
centralized.  This is more so as far as the cost sharing issue is concerned.    In a central 
Bill the cen tre must bear the major share of cost.  Many State Governments said that 
the entire cost should be born by the centre.  However the Committee has 
recommended categorization of States.  This is opening up a pandora‘s box and will 

 
 
 
lead to giving the centre e ven more powers to pick and choose the categories.  States 
with a particular problem of transportation such as the north -east States should get the 
benefit of the centre paying for the full amount.  In any case no cost sharing can be 
decided by central Gov ernment arbitrarily as is being done with many schemes and 
laws.  The relevant clauses in the Bill which deal with this topic must be deleted, 
otherwise it is extremely unfair to the States and moreover undermines the federal 
character of the constitution. 
5. On the issue of direct cash transfers the recommendation of the Committee is 
that ―at this juncture‖ it should not be introduced as the infrastructure is absent.  While 
this note of caution should be taken seriously by the Government, the Committee 
however has failed to recommend,   as it should  have, that reference to cash transfers 
in the Bill should be removed altogether.  I believe it is essential to emphasise in the 
recommendation that cash subsidy will not cover the entire cost of 35 kgs entItlem ent.  
Further, since there is no guarantee as to how the cash will be spent, it may lead to 
further malnutrition.  Retaining the cash transfer clause in the law would mean that a 
Government would have the legal right to enforce it. 
6. I think it is wrong f or the Committee to make a specific recommendation to limit 
the allowance of 1000 rupees to a pregnant woman for only the first two children.  This 
is imposing a two child norm which is objectionable.  The problems she face will be the 
same, if not more wi th a third child.  Why should we make the woman suffer, when she 
rarely has the power to take the decision about the numbers of children she should 
bear.  As far as anganwadis are concerned, the Committee has wrongly absolved the 
Government from providing free meals and added nutrition for pregnant mothers.  There 
are other points such as fixing control prices for pulses, edible oil and other essential 
commodities which should be included in the Bill. 
 
          Sd/- 
          DR. T.N. SEEMA, 
        MEMBER OF PARLIAMENT, 
          RAJYA SABHA
